replace Learning Goal-Conditioned Subgoals through Frozen Hierarchical World Models

Authors: Xiang Kaibo, Liu Qingfeng

Abstract: Hierarchical world models plan over long horizons by passing predicted states from a coarse model to a lower-level controller. Choosing these states can be expensive: the upper-level planner repeatedly differentiates through candidate macro-action trajectories. We replace this search with a lightweight goal-conditioned network trained through the frozen high-level predictor. The network supplies a conditioning vector, while the predictor generates the next latent waypoint. Training uses hindsight goals and state-prediction losses on offline trajectories, without search-solution targets or macro-action labels. Inference retains an autoregressive high-level rollout, but requires no high-level candidate optimization. We evaluate this approach on the shared-latent HWM checkpoints distributed with H-JEPA, using three backbone seeds and three head initializations per backbone. With a common evaluation protocol, mean success is 70.4% on Cube, 48.7% on Push-T, 100.0% on FourRoom, and 71.1% on Visual AntMaze. The method improves over the corresponding HWM search baseline on three environments and has the same mean on Push-T. Dedicated measurements on an NVIDIA L40S give 1.28-4.39x end-to-end speedups for batched evaluation. Ablations identify state-adaptive conditioning as a consistent contributor, while predictor-mediated subgoals and unrestricted conditions have environment-dependent benefits.

replace HGASurv: Hierarchical Granularity-Aware Multimodal Integration for Survival Prediction

Authors: Peike Yu, Wuchao Liu, Jihong Guan, Shuigeng Zhou

Abstract: Multimodal survival prediction jointly analyzes histopathological whole-slide images (WSIs), genomic profiles, and clinical variables to stratify patients by risk. However, existing approaches often inadequately account for granularity differences across modalities, particularly in spatial organization and feature dimensionality. Within pathology, aggregating WSI patch features without preserving their spatial arrangement can obscure tissue architecture; across modalities, directly concatenating low-dimensional clinical variables with high-dimensional visual and genomic features may limit the contribution of clinical information to prediction. To address these challenges, we propose HGASurv, which introduces a hierarchical WSI encoding module and a clinical-guided graph propagation module for granularity-aware multimodal integration. The hierarchical WSI encoding module maps WSI patch features onto a spatial grid using their original coordinates and applies parallel convolutions to construct representations at patch, local-neighborhood, and regional scales. Genomic signature embeddings then query these scale-specific WSI representations through cross-modal co-attention, producing signature-conditioned morphological features. The clinical-guided graph propagation module uses clinical variables to define patient-similarity edges and propagates fused image-genomic node embeddings with graph attention, integrating clinical information through topology rather than feature concatenation. Across three TCGA cohorts, HGASurv achieved the strongest overall C-index performance, with ablation studies supporting the complementary contributions of the hierarchical WSI encoding and clinical-guided graph propagation modules. This design shows that matching representation and integration mechanisms to modality-specific granularity improves multimodal survival prediction.

replace SpatialScout: Can VLM Agents Actively Explore and Map 3D Scenes?

Authors: Zhaoxi Zhang, Hao Liang, Linxuan Wu, Yiming Wu, Yifu Yuan, Kun Liang, Chenming Tang, Meiyi Qiang, Wentao Zhang, Weikang Li

Abstract: Spatial intelligence requires agents to actively acquire visual evidence and integrate observations into a coherent representation of the three-dimensional world. Yet spatial question answering offers limited evidence of whether models can construct accurate metric maps through autonomous exploration. We introduce SpatialScout, a benchmark for evaluating vision-language model (VLM) agents on active object-level 3D mapping. SpatialScout comprises 80 diverse indoor scenes containing 3,140 target objects. Given an object inventory, RGB observations, camera calibration, and camera poses, agents control a camera to explore each scene and estimate object centers in a shared coordinate frame. The task requires coordinating viewpoint selection, cross-view association, geometric estimation, and map revision under bounded interaction budgets. Evaluation jointly measures localization accuracy and target coverage through object-scale overlap scores that penalize missing predictions. Experiments with frontier VLMs reveal substantial limitations: the strongest evaluated model, Claude Opus 5.5, achieves a mean overlap score of 41.71%, with 47.23% of targets exceeding an intersection-over-union (IoU) threshold of 0.5 and only 3.25% exceeding 0.9. These results reveal a substantial gap between recovering coarse object layouts and constructing complete, precise metric maps. SpatialScout provides a quantitative testbed for developing agents that build reliable 3D spatial representations through active perception. Code is available at https://github.com/JesseZZZZZ/SpatialScout.

URLs: https://github.com/JesseZZZZZ/SpatialScout.

replace Open-Form Geometric Style Transfer

Authors: Adar Elad, Gal Metzer, Omer Dahary, Min Lu, Daniel Cohen-Or

Abstract: A geometric style learned from one family of shapes is typically applied to new shapes from the same family. We consider a more challenging setting: learning the geometric design language of a stylized Latin alphabet and applying it to new forms, from Greek glyphs to entirely different structures such as human poses. We introduce Open-Form Geometric Style Transfer, where the forms to which a learned geometric style can be applied are not restricted to the shape family in which that style was observed. This setting is deceptively challenging: the difficulty is not merely learning a style, but learning the style without learning the shape family in which it was observed. We represent target forms by structural sketches and disentangle structural adherence from style realization using a Geometry LoRA and a style-specific LoRA. Critically, open-form transfer requires the Geometry LoRA to be universal. To achieve this, we devise a geometry-enrichment strategy that constructs a large and purposefully diverse collection of synthetic structural sketches spanning a broad range of unfamiliar geometries. Our experiments and ablations demonstrate the importance of both disentanglement and this universal geometry prior, enabling geometric styles learned from glyphs, pictograms, and other shape families to be transferred to substantially different, unseen forms.

replace InfiniDream: Open-Ended Photorealistic 3D Worlds Through Procedural Generation

Authors: Yi-Chuan Huang, Hao-Jen Chien, Zheng-Hui Huang, Yu-Lun Liu, Kaipeng Zhang, Zhixiang Wang

Abstract: Procedural generation plays a key role in synthetic data creation and game development, offering greater controllability, editability, and diversity than static mesh representations. However, VLM-based code generation often produces relatively simple 3D assets, while sophisticated photorealistic procedural systems require extensive manual development. Existing VLM-guided procedural methods remain largely constrained by predefined factories and APIs, limiting their ability to create novel asset types. We introduce InfiniDream, a VLM-driven framework for open-ended procedural 3D world generation. First, we propose Symbolically Constrained Asset Generation, which leverages existing procedural algorithms to create novel, photorealistic assets while preserving editability and seed-based diversity. Second, we develop an end-to-end text-to-world pipeline that combines semantic scene planning with Deterministic Frustum-Guided Grid Placement to construct large-scale 3D worlds with precise object placement, procedural population, and animation. Extensive evaluations on 100 object and 20 scene generation tasks demonstrate improvements across four perceptual metrics. Diversity analysis, ablation studies, and human preference evaluations further validate the effectiveness of our framework. Project page: https://yichuanh.github.io/infinidream-page/

URLs: https://yichuanh.github.io/infinidream-page/

replace-cross Gibson Env: Real-World Perception for Embodied Agents

Authors: Fei Xia, Amir Zamir, Zhi-Yang He, Alexander Sax, Jitendra Malik, Silvio Savarese

Abstract: Developing visual perception models for active agents and sensorimotor control are cumbersome to be done in the physical world, as existing algorithms are too slow to efficiently learn in real-time and robots are fragile and costly. This has given rise to learning-in-simulation which consequently casts a question on whether the results transfer to real-world. In this paper, we are concerned with the problem of developing real-world perception for active agents, propose Gibson Virtual Environment for this purpose, and showcase sample perceptual tasks learned therein. Gibson is based on virtualizing real spaces, rather than using artificially designed ones, and currently includes over 1400 floor spaces from 572 full buildings. The main characteristics of Gibson are: I. being from the real-world and reflecting its semantic complexity, II. having an internal synthesis mechanism, "Goggles", enabling deploying the trained models in real-world without needing further domain adaptation, III. embodiment of agents and making them subject to constraints of physics and space.

replace-cross WAFFLE: Finetuning Multi-Modal Models for Automated Front-End Development

Authors: Shanchao Liang, Nan Jiang, Shangshu Qian, Lin Tan

Abstract: Web development involves turning UI designs into functional webpages, which can be difficult for both beginners and experienced developers due to the complexity of HTML's hierarchical structures and styles. While Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown promise in generating source code, two major challenges persist in UI-to-HTML code generation: (1) effectively representing HTML's hierarchical structure for LLMs, and (2) bridging the gap between the visual nature of UI designs and the text-based format of HTML code. To tackle these challenges, we introduce Waffle, a new fine-tuning strategy that uses a structure-aware attention mechanism to improve LLMs' understanding of HTML's structure and a contrastive fine-tuning approach to align LLMs' understanding of UI images and HTML code. Models fine-tuned with Waffle show up to 9.00 pp (percentage point) higher HTML match, 0.0982 higher CW-SSIM, 32.99 higher CLIP, and 27.12 pp higher LLEM on our new benchmark WebSight-Test and an existing benchmark Design2Code, outperforming current fine-tuning methods.

replace-cross PALATE: Peculiar Application of the Law of Total Expectation to Enhance the Evaluation of Deep Generative Models

Authors: Tadeusz Dziarmaga, Marcin K\k{a}dzio{\l}ka, Laura Musio{\l}, Patryk Marsza{\l}ek, Artur Kasymov, Piotr Ko\'scielniak, Marcin Mazur

Abstract: Deep generative models (DGMs) have caused a paradigm shift in the field of machine learning, yielding noteworthy advancements in domains such as image synthesis, natural language processing, and other related areas. However, a comprehensive evaluation of these models that effectively captures the interplay among fidelity, diversity, and novelty in generated samples remains a persistent challenge. A recently proposed approach, the Feature Likelihood Divergence (FLD), offers a theoretically grounded and practical tool in this regard but faces notable computational limitations. In this paper, we propose PALATE, a novel framework designed to enhance the evaluation of DGMs by addressing the efficiency constraints of FLD. Our approach is based on a peculiar application of the law of total expectation to random variables representing accessible data. When integrated with the MMD baseline metric and the recently introduced DINOv3 feature extractor, PALATE provides a comprehensive evaluation framework that matches or exceeds state-of-the-art methods while offering superior computational efficiency and scalability to large-scale datasets. Through a series of experiments, we demonstrate that the PALATE enhancement constitutes a practical and computationally efficient framework for holistic DGM assessment, particularly in detecting sample memorization and evaluating generalization capabilities. The source code is provided as supplementary material.

replace-cross SCOPE: Scalable Training-Free Semantic Coreset Selection for Long-Tailed Federated Learning

Authors: Md Anwar Hossen, Nathan R. Tallent, Luanzheng Guo, Ali Jannesary

Abstract: Scientific discovery increasingly relies on federated learning over data from high-resolution instruments, and these data are strongly non-IID and long-tailed. Coreset selection could shorten training, but existing federated coreset methods either prune with local heuristics that ignore the global class distribution, or first train a warm-up model at every client and upload class centroids of its features. This makes coreset selection a distributed bottleneck that consumes GPU time at every client, grows with the model and the number of clients, and must be repeated for every architecture. We introduce SCOPE, named for Semantic Coreset using Orthogonal Projection Embeddings, a scalable, training-free federated coreset framework for long-tailed data. Each client scores its samples once with a frozen, compact vision-language model and uploads scalar statistics per class. The server merges these into a global class profile, which lets each client remove anomalies and prune redundant samples only from classes that are locally abundant but globally common. SCOPE therefore requires no warm-up training, needs a single exchange with the server, and uploads a payload that depends only on the number of classes. On GPU nodes of an HPC cluster, with four datasets, including scientific micrographs, and six baselines, SCOPE selects its coreset up to 7.7x faster and uploads up to 293x less data than the feature-based baseline. As the number of clients grows from 10 to 500, SCOPE's time before training grows by fewer seconds than any baseline's, and at 500 clients the baselines need 1.7 to 3.6x SCOPE's time. Trained on the coreset, the global model reaches 98% of the full-data accuracy in 18 to 42% fewer global rounds than full-data training, with 8 to 21% less client computation over 200 rounds.

replace-cross StippleDiffusion: Capacity-Constrained Stippling using Controlled Diffusion

Authors: Ofir Gilad, Aleksander Plocharski, Przemyslaw Musialski, Andrei Sharf

Abstract: Stipple patterns, point sets whose local density tracks a target image, are traditionally produced by per-density iterative optimizers, which are slow, non-differentiable, and must be re-run from scratch for each new target. Learned alternatives have so far addressed only unconditional point generation; capacity-constrained, image-conditioned stippling has remained out of reach. We present the first diffusion-based sampler that simultaneously satisfies a learned local point-distribution prior and a continuous, image-defined capacity constraint at inference. The method is a ControlNet branch built on top of an optimal-transport-grid point-set diffusion baseline, conditioned on the target density map and a high-resolution image. Two design choices make the combination tractable: training and inference are restricted to the late-stage denoising regime, initialized from a density-weighted rejection sample, and the standard zero-convolution injection is replaced with a sigmoid-gated 1x1 projection that preserves the base model's blue-noise structure under hard density signals. A single trained checkpoint accepts arbitrary target densities at inference, generalizes to point budgets that were not seen during training, and produces stipples in time nearly independent of the output point count. On the Icons-50 benchmark, our learned sampler reaches parity with per-density-optimized baselines on every reported metric while remaining differentiable end-to-end.

replace-cross DiffEEG: A Self-Supervised Denoising Diffusion Model for Learning EEG seizure-related Representations

Authors: Abdulkader Helwan, Lina Abou-Abbas, Hussein El Amouri, Belkacem Chikhaoui, Khadidja Henni

Abstract: Deep learning for EEG-based seizure detection faces critical challenges: severe annotation scarcity and extreme class imbalance, where ictal events comprise less than 10\% of clinical recordings. We present DiffEEG, a 9.6M-parameter self-supervised foundation model that addresses both limitations through denoising diffusion pre-training and reinforcement learning (RL)-based fine-tuning. Pre-trained on 1.3M unlabeled segments from the Temple University Hospital Seizure Corpus (TUHSZ), DiffEEG learns generic neural representations via a 1D U-Net with multi-head self-attention. For downstream adaptation, a reinforced decision layer employs policy gradient optimization to directly maximize F1-score, prioritizing sensitivity to rare seizure events over overall accuracy. Under strict patient-wise evaluation (279 patients, Leave-One-Fold-Out), DiffEEG achieves 61\% accuracy and 59\% F1 for 4-class seizure subtyping, and 81\% accuracy with 85\% weighted F1 for binary detection, maintaining clinically viable seizure recall (59\%) despite extreme imbalance (6.7\% prevalence). Segment-level evaluation establishes an upper bound of 97.6\% accuracy, confirming strong architectural capacity. DiffEEG demonstrates that diffusion-based pre-training combined with metric-aware reinforcement learning enables clinically deployable seizure monitoring with minimal labeled data requirements.

replace-cross TokenSwap: Benchmarking and Reducing the Modality Gap in Multimodal LLMs

Authors: Andong Hua, Colton Bishop, Igor Mordatch, Arian Hosseini, Jindong Gu, Aleksandra Faust, Rebecca Roelofs, Yao Qin

Abstract: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) should exhibit cross-modal consistency, generating consistent responses given semantically equivalent inputs across modalities. However, we observe a systematic discrepancy in model predictions under such cross-modal variations. Specifically, we define the modality gap as the difference in model performance under semantically equivalent textual and multimodal inputs. We introduce TokenSwap, a method that constructs such inputs by replacing textual concepts with semantically aligned images, resulting in sequences where visual tokens are interleaved with text tokens. Based on TokenSwap, we transform existing text-based benchmarks (e.g., MMLU) into image-interleaved counterparts, resulting in TokenSwap-Bench. Across 42 MLLMs, we observe a pervasive modality gap, with performance decreasing by 4.2% to 47.4% when moving from text-only to image-interleaved inputs, averaging 19.6% +/- 3.3% across models. Notably, reasoning models exhibit consistently smaller gaps, achieving an average gap of 10.1% compared to 25.5% for non-reasoning models, suggesting greater robustness in multimodal reasoning across equivalent textual and visual representations. In contrast, neither prompting strategies nor scaling training compute alone reliably reduces the modality gap. Finally, we demonstrate that incorporating TokenSwap during training effectively mitigates this gap, improving cross-modal alignment while preserving strong text-only and vision-language model performance. The project page is available at https://dongxzz.github.io/TokenSwap/.

URLs: https://dongxzz.github.io/TokenSwap/.

replace-cross ReRef-3D: A Benchmark for Spatial Referring Expression-Guided 3D Object Placement

Authors: Mary Lynn Martin, Yifei Zhang, Martha Palmer, Maria Leonor Pacheco

Abstract: We introduce ReRef-3D, a benchmark for language-guided single-object placement in controlled 3D tabletop scenes. It contains 33,826 instructions across 998 CLEVR-derived scenes, spanning 16 placement types and direct, one-hop, and two-hop references. Given that an instruction defines a region of acceptable placements rather than one coordinate, predictions are evaluated by relation satisfaction and physical validity. Each instruction also includes a verified natural rephrasing. We evaluate LLaVA-3D, 3D-LLM, 3D-LLaVA, LL3DA, and PlaceIt3D, with PlaceIt3D evaluated under both point-based and region-based supervision. Region-supervised PlaceIt3D achieves the highest placement accuracy at about 80% and LLaVA-3D reaches about 70%. Performance varies by relation type, but nearest and aligned remain the most difficult. Rephrasing has little effect on performance overall.

replace-cross Harnessing Magnitude-Only and Complex Measurements for Improved Dynamic MRI Reconstruction with Learned Priors

Authors: Mahdi Saberi, Ya\c{s}ar Utku Al\c{c}alar, Merve G\"{u}lle, Chetan Shenoy, Mehmet Ak\c{c}akaya

Abstract: MRI reconstruction methods for undersampled k-space data naturally utilize complex-valued measurements. Parallel developments in sparse phase retrieval have shown that magnitude-only measurements may provide complementary information for signal recovery. However, their use in MRI reconstruction remains largely unexplored, due to lack of practical settings where informative magnitude measurements can be obtained without additional scan time. In this work, we investigate the use of auxiliary k-space magnitude information for accelerated steady-state dynamic MRI reconstruction, and demonstrate strong consistency of k-space magnitudes across time-frames. Building on this observation, we propose $\mathbb{C}+\text{Mag}$, a magnitude-informed physics-driven deep learning reconstruction method. The proposed method employs an ADMM-based unrolling framework with a novel magnitude-aware data-fidelity formulation, where quadratically smoothed optimization and momentum-based updates are introduced to address the non-differentiability and non-convexity of the magnitude constraints. Experiments on retrospectively undersampled cine MRI and phase-contrast flow MRI datasets, as well as prospectively undersampled real-time cine MRI acquisitions, demonstrate improved artifact suppression, sharper anatomical recovery, and better preservation of phase information compared to conventional PD-DL methods, which is further supported through blinded expert reader evaluations.

replace-cross $S^3$: Spectral Null-Space Swap Makes Reasoning Models Efficient

Authors: Hongbo Ma, Sansheng Cao, Jiajun Fan, Bangji Yang, Ge Liu

Abstract: LLMs trained with Chain-of-thought excel in reasoning capability, but often come with excessive token cost. We find that the core of reasoning capacity lies in the Thinking model's weight component within the null space of a projection defined by the corresponding Non-thinking model's dominant singular directions, and removing the subspace component can largely improve reasoning efficiency without hurting the accuracy gained during thinking-mode post-training. Unlike existing efforts that mostly operate within the dominant subspace, we are the first to unveil the critical role of the null space and harness it for model optimization. Motivated by this finding, we propose Spectral Null-Space Swap ($S^3$), a training-free composition of paired Non-thinking and Thinking checkpoints. Our method keeps the Non-thinking model inside its own dominant subspace and takes the Thinking checkpoint outside it, improving reasoning efficiency while maintaining accuracy. We extensively evaluate $S^3$ on 2B-30B dense and mixture-of-experts (MoE) architectures spanning 28 evaluation environments across mathematical, multimodal, and audio reasoning domains. $S^3$ establishes new empirical Pareto Frontiers among training-free model composition strategies: across all settings, it reduces inference token overhead by an average of 27.4% compared to full Thinking models while simultaneously improving overall task accuracy by 1.0 percentage point (e.g., yielding +8.3% accuracy on HMMT25 alongside a 33.0% token speedup). We further use attention entropy for explanation and find that the retained component produces more concentrated attention, and we use a simplified analytical model about optimization to demonstrate why null-space can effectively reduce attention entropy, thereby improving the efficiency of reasoning.

replace-cross MultiWorldBench: Do Independently Controlled Views Describe One Shared World?

Authors: Zhangbo Xu, Ruoxi Zhang, Rui Hu, Yisong Wang, Xitong Li, Jingdong Wang

Abstract: Multiplayer world models must ensure that independently controlled views remain consistent with a single shared and persistent world. We introduce MultiWorldBench, a diagnostic Minecraft benchmark comprising 495 case configurations across seven task suites and ten capabilities, including independent control, cross-view motion, shared-state synchronization, persistence, structural reasoning, concurrent interaction, and delayed revisit. Using Engine GT as a reference, we evaluate two multiplayer world models, Solaris (MP) and Gamma-World, together with a single-player generation variant, Solaris (SP). Among the generated systems, Gamma-World achieves the highest ten-capability average at 21.39, followed by Solaris (MP) at 20.88 and Solaris (SP) at 10.06, while Engine GT reaches 91.69. Gamma-World performs better on several control, shared-state, and revisit capabilities, whereas Solaris (MP) leads in cross-view motion and race-condition consistency. Nevertheless, all generated systems score at most 8.00 on state persistence and 1.33 on structural consistency, and none succeeds in spatial reasoning or building-identity preservation. Human pairwise evaluation yields the same overall system ranking and shows strong alignment with the automatic evaluation, with a mean dimension-level Spearman correlation of 0.95. These results show that current multiplayer world models still exhibit a substantial gap relative to Engine GT in maintaining a coherent shared world.

replace-cross ECGWmark: Deep Watermarking of Electrocardiogram Signals With Error-Correcting Codes

Authors: Kuan-Yu Chen, Kuan-Chen Wang, Yu Tsao, Jian-Jiun Ding

Abstract: Wearable electrocardiogram (ECG) recordings pass through multiple networks and systems before automated interpretation, making patient metadata vulnerable to loss. Digital watermarking embeds this metadata directly into the signal. However, conventional ECG watermarking methods fail under temporal desynchronization, and waveform similarity does not guarantee downstream diagnostic preservation. This article presents ECGWmark, a deep watermarking framework for single-lead ECG. A fully convolutional generator embeds the payload without signal quantization, a detector decodes the message frame by frame and verifies watermark presence, and a frozen arrhythmia classifier penalizes changes that would alter diagnostic predictions. To bridge the gap between bit accuracy and whole-message recovery, payloads are protected by extended Hamming codes, validation-selected interleaving, and soft-decision decoding. On the MIT-BIH Arrhythmia Database, ECGWmark maintains over 90\% bit accuracy under time shifts of 10 to 50 samples, where transform baselines fall to chance level. With the coding layer, the protected 16-bit payload achieves 99.86\% exact recovery on clean segments and sustains over 90\% recovery under time shifts, 10\% sample deletion, and 10\% packet loss. Inter-patient evaluation confirms improved ventricular-beat sensitivity with preserved macro-F1. Requiring only 0.56 million parameters and about 15~ms per segment on a desktop CPU, ECGWmark highlights the need to assess physiological watermarking through whole-message recovery and diagnostic consistency.

replace-cross Deficit-Driven Stratified Acquisition for Building STRATA-S12: An Open SAR-Optical Corpus for Self-Supervised Learning

Authors: Christian Sanchez-Leon, Dongming Chen, Dongqi Wang

Abstract: Building a co-registered SAR-optical corpus for self-supervised Earth Observation is above all an acquisition problem, yet the main multi-modal datasets are collected opportunistically, with no per-stratum quota control and no mechanism against the geographic dropout that non-uniform cloud cover leaves behind. We present Deficit-Driven Stratified Acquisition (DDSA), an algorithm that treats building a co-registered Sentinel-1/Sentinel-2 corpus as a constrained maximum-coverage problem over a hemisphere-corrected (class x season x region) stratification, enforces strict temporal pairing and a multi-stage quality gate, and closes the loop during acquisition with a human-in-the-loop deficit-repair stage. Measured on its own product, the algorithm yields three results a single-pass acquisition cannot report. Deficit-driven ordering makes a partial run a covering one: it holds every one of the 192 strata populated from the first few percent of the budget, where an abundance-first order leaves most strata empty until the end. Whether a failed attempt can be recovered depends on the temporal persistence of its cause, not the kind of defect: geometric failures such as nodata are among the most repairable (eta = 41.4%) while cloud is among the least (eta = 18.8%), because a cloudy location is cloudy on most dates of its season. The repair loop supplies 39.5% of the released pairs at no measurable quality cost, with 89.8% surviving manual review. The quality guarantee has a price: 2,928,370 acquisition attempts produced 740,746 released pairs. We release the corpus, STRATA-S12, with STAC/PDS4-aligned FAIR sidecars, and a pre-registered linear-probe check confirms it is fit for self-supervised pre-training. A metadata-level coverage comparison against SSL4EO-S12 and SEN12MS situates the corpus over a shared frame; systematic cross-corpus and cross-method comparison is left to follow-up work.

replace-cross Thinker-WM: A Unified World Model with Shared Multimodal Representations for Embodied Intelligence

Authors: Thinker Team, Peng Gao, Jichao Jiao, Chen Liao, Daqin Luo, Yiming Ma, Baiyu Pan, Hailin Shi, Jiyuan Wang, Xiangming Xu, Xiongwei Zhao

Abstract: World models have emerged as a promising paradigm for embodied AI, yet existing systems often remain fragmented in the modalities they model and the functions they support. In particular, visual dynamics, geometric structure, and executable action are commonly coupled through separate interfaces or downstream modules rather than being jointly represented and evolved within a shared dynamics model. In this work, we present Thinker-WM, a unified multimodal world model that jointly models and generates RGB, metric depth, and robot action through a shared intermediate Unified Dynamics Transformer with modality-specific encoders and decoders. The model performs multimodal unification at the representation level: appearance, geometry, and action are transformed into a common dynamic representation and jointly evolved by shared parameters, while their distinct output structures are preserved. The same parameterization supports multiple functions, including joint video-action generation, world prediction, policy inference, action-conditioned generation, video-to-action generation, and inverse dynamics. To examine whether the shared representation captures action-related information, we introduce a lightweight motion probe that shows the frozen visual representation contains recoverable motion information. Thinker-WM is trained on a heterogeneous corpus comprising approximately 20K hours of egocentric video, 10K hours of real-robot data, and 1K hours of simulation data, including approximately 4K hours of real-robot data with aligned depth supervision. Extensive experiments demonstrate strong performance across embodied world-model evaluations. Thinker-WM ranks first on EWMBench, WorldOlympiad and WorldArena, while controlled studies further demonstrate the benefits of action conditioning, geometric interaction, and multi-function training within the shared representation.

replace-cross Alignment of visual brain encoding models with individual perceptual similarity judgments

Authors: Daniel Chong, Haomiao Chen, Keith W Jamison, Raja Marjieh, Ilia Sucholutsky, Nori Jacoby, Amy Kuceyeski

Abstract: Encoding models that predict brain activation responses to images have traditionally been trained to maximize accuracy when predicting observed brain data. However, their internal representations may not reflect individual human perception. In this work, we investigate whether we can fine-tune brain encoding models to better align their representations with individuals' similarity judgments. Using similarity judgments of 100 images from 8 participants in the Natural Scenes Dataset (NSD), we adapt a generic human similarity judgment model (DreamSim) separately for each participant to obtain personalized perceptual similarity models. These models are then used to obtain subject-specific similarity structure across images in their encoding model training sets. We fine-tune each participant's baseline encoder in two conditions, aligning its representations with either generic or personalized similarity judgments, with the same initialization and objective. We find that baseline brain encoders reflect within-subject perceptual similarity more strongly than measured fMRI responses and preserve intersubject correlations present in measured fMRI, but fail to reflect those of human perceptual similarity judgments. Fine-tuning with generic, population-level perceptual similarity information results in better alignment with human judgments than baseline encoders, but these models still fail to capture intersubject correlations of human judgments. However, fine-tuning with personalized perceptual similarity information results in models that are the most aligned with human perceptual similarity judgments and also reflect intersubject correlations of human judgments. Our framework provides an approach for bootstrapping human similarity judgment data into more individualized, human-aligned brain encoding models of vision.

replace-cross Implicit Neural Models for Direct Multi-Shell Diffusion MRI Signal Estimation

Authors: Sanna Persson, Fabian Leander Sinzinger, Christoffer Olsson, Rodrigo Moreno

Abstract: Diffusion magnetic resonance imaging is a noninvasive technique that is sensitive to the motion of water molecules in human tissue. The image signal is commonly described in q-space; however, a complete coverage of this space is often infeasible due to hardware and acquisition time limitations. In this work, we propose to represent the entire diffusion signal using an implicit neural representation network with wavelet activation functions. The signal space is encoded using Fourier features and spherical harmonics basis functions for a continuous output distribution of the raw diffusion data, enabling both super-resolution and q-space interpolation. Additionally, we introduce a pretraining strategy using a group template, enabling faster per-subject optimisation when combined with nonlinear registrations of subjects into template space. We tested the method with 10 subjects from the Human Connectome Project and 26 subjects from in-house data (multidimensional diffusion MRI from a Parkinson's disease cohort). The results show that the proposed method yields high-fidelity reconstructions with significantly reduced memory requirements compared to raw diffusion-weighted images. The pretraining scheme showed good generalisability properties on data acquired with very different protocols. We further assess the method on downstream metrics relevant to diffusion MRI data from the learned representations.

replace-cross InteractAgent: Agentic Human Motion Interaction with Memory-Augmented Reflection

Authors: Senhe Zhang, Tao Zhang, Yujia Zhang, Zeyu Zhang, Dong Gong

Abstract: Long-horizon human-scene interaction requires coordinated spatial reasoning, physical feasibility, and the ability to recover from compounding execution errors. Text-driven motion models are largely scene-agnostic and lack closed-loop correction, while geometry-conditioned approaches depend on paired scene-motion training or explicit 3D preprocessing that limits generalization to novel layouts and open-vocabulary goals. We present InteractAgent, an agentic multimodal LLM system for long-horizon human-scene interaction that plans, executes, observes, and reflects in a closed loop, using a single MLLM as both planner and evaluator and a frozen MotionLLM as the executor. InteractAgent integrates Online Observation for step-wise recovery decisions (continue/redo/replan) and Memory-Augmented Reflection for multi-round plan refinement with adaptive stopping. On UniHSI, InteractAgent improves task success and reduces penetration compared with training-free baselines. Code: https://github.com/AIGeeksGroup/InteractAgent.

replace-cross Text-to-Image Generative Modeling Without Language Pretraining

Authors: Keshigeyan Chandrasegaran, Yue Zhao, Armin W. Thomas, Ehsan Adeli, Jiajun Wu, Juan Carlos Niebles, Li Fei-Fei, Michael Poli

Abstract: Modern text-to-image (T2I) systems use internet-scale language pretraining for text-conditioned image generation. We ask whether language pretraining is necessary for strong T2I generation. We introduce Halcyon, a family of early-fusion multimodal autoregressive models pretrained entirely from scratch on GPIC, a dataset of 100M image-text pairs. Halcyon-0.52B outperforms LlamaGen-XL/8 (48.0 vs. 63.3 gFD) with 18.2$\times$ less pretraining compute. Controlled experiments show that text-only pretraining does not improve T2I generation. Halcyon also learns image captioning by replacing caption dropout with I2T supervision, maintaining competitive T2I generation quality under the same pretraining budget. We show that strong T2I systems can be trained from scratch without internet-scale language pretraining. Code and models are available at https://cs.stanford.edu/~keshik/halcyon
